9/21/23, 10:20 - Nikhil Sharma: #include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stdbool.h>

bool isValidIdentifier(const char* str) {
    if (strlen(str) == 0) {
        return false;  // Empty string is not a valid identifier.
    }

    // Check if the first character is a letter or underscore.
    if (!((str[0] >= 'a' && str[0] <= 'z') || (str[0] >= 'A' && str[0] <= 'Z') || str[0] == '_')) {
        return false;
    }

    // Check the remaining characters.
    for (int i = 1; i < strlen(str); i++) {
        if (!((str[i] >= 'a' && str[i] <= 'z') || (str[i] >= 'A' && str[i] <= 'Z') ||
              (str[i] >= '0' && str[i] <= '9') || str[i] == '_')) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    // Check if it's a C keyword.
    char keywords[][20] = {
        "auto", "break", "case", "char", "const", "continue", "default", "do", "double",
        "else", "enum", "extern", "float", "for", "goto", "if", "int", "long", "register",
        "return", "short", "signed", "sizeof", "static", "struct", "switch", "typedef", "union",
        "unsigned", "void", "volatile", "while"
    };

    int numKeywords = sizeof(keywords) / sizeof(keywords[0]);
    for (int i = 0; i < numKeywords; i++) {
        if (strcmp(str, keywords[i]) == 0) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    return true;
}

int main() {
    char input[100];

    printf("Enter a string to check if it's a valid C identifier: ");
    scanf("%s", input);

    if (isValidIdentifier(input)) {
        printf("%s is a valid C identifier.\n", input);
    } else {
        printf("%s is not a valid C identifier.\n", input);
    }

    return 0;
}
9/21/23, 10:21 - Nikhil Sharma: #include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
int main()
{
    char str[100];
    int i, words = 0, chars = 0, lines = 0;
    printf("Enter a string: ");
    gets(str);
    for (i = 0; str[i] != '\0'; i++)
    {
        chars++;
        if (str[i] == ' ')
        {
            words++;
        }
        if (str[i] == '\n')
        {
            lines++;
        }
    }
    printf("Number of words: %d\n", words);
    printf("Number of characters: %d\n", chars);
    printf("Number of lines: %d\n", lines);
    return 0;
}

9/28/23, 22:52 - Aman Tiwari: *Important Information*

GGSIPU New Delhi invites online applications for the *EWS scholarship (AY 2022-23) under category 1* on September 25, 2023.

EWS scholarships will be announced in two categories:

*Category 1:* Those applicants with an NFS Card (National Food Security-Ration Card) are eligible for the EWS scholarship announced on September 25, 2023. Also, applicants have to produce proof of *current 6-month entries on the NFS Card* along with the other proofs mentioned in the guidelines.

*NOTE:*
•	Applicants have to upload an image of their NFS card on the portal under the income certificate proof option shown on the online application form.
•	Applicants with pending backlogs for (AY 2021-22) are not eligible for the scholarship.
•	Applicants have to submit a hard copy of the application after it is verified by the nodal officer.
•	At the time of submission of the hard copy, the applicant must bring all the original documents used to fill out the online application form.
•	A hard copy of the application is verified by the DSW, GGSIPU, New Delhi.

 *Category 2:* Those applicants only having an *income certificate (less than 2.5 lacs) are not eligible* for the current EWS scholarship announced on September 25, 2023. If the fund is left after declaring the list of successful applicants for category 1, then the *university will invite online applications for the EWS scholarship (AY 2022-23) under category 2*

For more queries please contact us on *(Only for DTC Students)*:

2. Industry Demand: With advancements in technology and increasing industrialization, there is a high demand for skilled engineers across various sectors. B.Tech graduates from DTU can find employment in fields such as IT, software development, electronics, communication, civil engineering, mechanical engineering, and more.

3. Entrepreneurship: Along with employment opportunities, a B.Tech degree from DTU equips students with the necessary knowledge and skills to start their own ventures. The university promotes entrepreneurship and offers resources and support for aspiring entrepreneurs.

4. Higher Education: Many B.Tech graduates opt for higher studies after completing their degree. DTU provides a solid foundation for pursuing postgraduate degrees like M.Tech, MBA, or other research-oriented programs.

5. Research and Development: DTU emphasizes research and innovation. B.Tech graduates can explore opportunities in research and development (R&D) organizations, both in academia and industry. They can contribute to cutting-edge technological advancements and work on solving complex problems.

6. Global Opportunities: The reputation of DTU extends beyond national boundaries. B.Tech graduates from DTU have opportunities to work globally in reputed companies or pursue higher education in esteemed international universities.

It is important to note that career prospects also depend on individual skills, performance, and specialization within the field of engineering. Keeping up with emerging technologies, gaining practical experience through internships, and building a strong network can further enhance career opportunities.

The scope of a Bachelor of Technology (B.Tech) degree from Delhi Technological University (DTC) is quite promising. Here are some aspects to consider:

1. Job Opportunities: DTC is known for its strong engineering programs, which opens up a wide range of job opportunities for B.Tech graduates. The university has a good reputation among recruiters, and many leading companies visit the campus for placements.

2. Industry Demand: With advancements in technology and increasing industrialization, there is a high demand for skilled engineers across various sectors. B.Tech graduates from DTC can find employment in fields such as IT, software development, electronics, communication, civil engineering, mechanical engineering, and more.

3. Entrepreneurship: Along with employment opportunities, a B.Tech degree from DTC equips students with the necessary knowledge and skills to start their own ventures. The university promotes entrepreneurship and offers resources and support for aspiring entrepreneurs.

4. Higher Education: Many B.Tech graduates opt for higher studies after completing their degree. DTC provides a solid foundation for pursuing postgraduate degrees like M.Tech, MBA, or other research-oriented programs.

5. Research and Development: DTC emphasizes research and innovation. B.Tech graduates can explore opportunities in research and development (R&D) organizations, both in academia and industry. They can contribute to cutting-edge technological advancements and work on solving complex problems.

6. Global Opportunities: The reputation of DTC extends beyond national boundaries. B.Tech graduates from DTC have opportunities to work globally in reputed companies or pursue higher education in esteemed international universities.

It is important to note that career prospects also depend on individual skills, performance, and specialization within the field of engineering. Keeping up with emerging technologies, gaining practical experience through internships, and building a strong network can further enhance career opportunities.

1/8/24, 22:24 - Saiyam: Sure, with a subnet mask of 255.255.255.224:

The subnet mask consists of 32 bits in total. In this case, 27 bits are used for the network (because 255.255.255.224 translates to 11111111.11111111.11111111.11100000 in binary), leaving 5 bits for hosts.

1. Host bits & Subnet bits:
   - There are 5 host bits (32 - 27).

2. Number of subnets:
   - With 5 subnet bits, there are 2^5 = 32 subnets (2 raised to the power of the number of subnet bits).

3. Number of hosts per subnet:
   - With 5 host bits, there are 2^5 - 2 = 30 hosts per subnet (2 raised to the power of the number of host bits minus 2 for the network address and broadcast address).

4. Total number of hosts in the network:
   - The total number of hosts can be calculated by multiplying the number of hosts per subnet by the number of subnets:
     Total hosts = Number of subnets * Number of hosts per subnet
                 = 32 * 30
                 = 960

Therefore, in this network configuration, there are 5 bits for host addresses, allowing for 32 subnets, each accommodating 30 hosts, resulting in a total of 960 hosts in the entire network.

1/8/24, 22:58 - +91 70606 96779: The formula for calculating the number of subnets in a network depends on the subnet mask and the address space provided. The formula is given by:

Number of Subnets
=
2
(
Number of Subnet Bits
)
Number of Subnets=2 
(Number of Subnet Bits)
 

Here, the "Number of Subnet Bits" represents the number of bits used for subnetting. If you have a subnet mask in CIDR (Classless Inter-Domain Routing) notation, the subnet bits are the additional bits beyond the standard network bits.

For example, consider a subnet mask in CIDR notation as "/n," where "n" represents the number of network bits. The remaining bits (32 - n) are used for subnetting. Therefore, the formula becomes:

Number of Subnets
=
2
(
32
−
�
)
Number of Subnets=2 
(32−n)
 

This formula calculates the number of subnets based on the subnetting scheme used in IPv4 networking. Each additional bit used for subnetting doubles the number of available subnets. Keep in mind that the actual number of usable subnets may be slightly less due to reserved addresses, such as the network and broadcast addresses in each subnet.

1/8/24, 23:02 - +91 70606 96779: For the given subnet mask address 255.255.255.224, let's analyze the subnetting details:

Subnet Mask Analysis:
The subnet mask 255.255.255.224 in binary is 11111111.11111111.11111111.11100000.

a) Host Bits & Subnet Bits:
Host Bits: The number of bits available for hosts is determined by the number of trailing zeros in the subnet mask. In this case, there are 5 trailing zeros (in the last octet), so there are 5 host bits.
Subnet Bits: The remaining bits are used for subnetting. In this case, there are 32 - 5 = 27 subnet bits.
b) Number of Subnets & Number of Hosts per Subnet:
Number of Subnets: The number of subnets is determined by 2^(number of subnet bits). In this case, it's 2^27 = 134,217,728 subnets.
Number of Hosts per Subnet: The number of hosts per subnet is determined by 2^(number of host bits) - 2 (subtracting 2 for network and broadcast addresses). In this case, it's 2^5 - 2 = 30 hosts per subnet.
c) Total Number of Hosts in the Network:
To find the total number of hosts in the network, you multiply the number of hosts per subnet by the number of subnets:
Total Number of Hosts=Number of Hosts per Subnet×Number of Subnets
Total Number of Hosts=Number of Hosts per Subnet×Number of Subnets
Total Number of Hosts=30×134,217,728
Total Number of Hosts=30×134,217,728
Total Number of Hosts=4,026831840
Total Number of Hosts=4,026,831,840

So, for the given subnet mask 255.255.255.224:

a) There are 5 host bits and 27 subnet bits.
b) There are 134,217,728 subnets, and each subnet can accommodate 30 hosts.
c) The total number of hosts in the network is 4,026,831,840
